## p.1

THERAPEUTICS 1

                                                             Week 1: Common cold & Influenza

• Common cold vs. influenza                                                        • Mini case study: cold or flu?
  s Attack by pathogenic wind in East Asian medicine, and virus in Western           s A mother calls you up asking for advice. Her 11 year old daughter has had a

     medicine                                                                           104° F for three days, brought down to 102° w/ acetaminophen. Her Sx
  s Clinical signs and sx include nasal discharge, sneezing, headache, chills,
                                                                                        include severe fatigue, aches and chills. She doesn’t want to take her child
     fever, fatigue, body aches.
  s More severe signs and sx associated w/ influenza (caused by a different virus       to the doctor for just a cold.
                                                                                        § What do you think? Is it probably cold or flu? Flu
     than the common cold)                                                              § How would you advise this person? Go to the doctor [Dehydration: Fever

• Cold Viruses                                                                             for more than 3 days]
  s Rhinoviruses, coronaviruses, and the respiratory syncytial virus (RSV)
  s Rhinovirus group causes 10% to 40% of colds                                    • Mini case study: flu vaccination
     § Most active in early fall, spring, and summer                                 s For several months you’ve been treating a four year old boy with respiratory
     § More than 110 distinct rhinovirus types have been identified
     § Grow best at temperatures of about 91 degrees                                    problems. He has a Dx of bronchomalacia, and an Hx of frequent
  s Coronaviruses and rsv responsible for 20% and 10% of cases, respectively.
  s Coronaviruses cause many adult colds                                                pneumonia. Flu season is coming up, and his parents want to know if they
     § Most active in the winter and early spring                                       should have him get a flu vaccine.
     § Three or four of the 30+ types infect humans                                     § What do you think? No
     § SARS is due to a coronavirus                                                     § What do you say? Yes, because liability
                                                                                        § What might you say if you were an ND or MD/L.Ac.? Yes
• Influenza viruses
  s Three types, A, B, and C                                                       • TCM etiology and pathogenesis
  s A and B most severe, and change continually, circulate around the globe          s Two broad categories
  s C mild or asymptomatic, does not cause epidemics and does not present a             § Pathogenic wind

     public health threat                                                                  • Air travel

• Drift and shift                                                                          • Closed ventilation office buildings
  s Antigenic drift
     § Small changes that happen continually                                               • Hotel pillows and blankets
     § Produces new strains
     § Yearly updates in the influenza vaccine address changes d/t drift                   • Playgrounds, classrooms
     § Affects both A and B types                                                       § Deficiency syndromes
  s Antigenic shift
     § Sudden major change in the influenza A viruses                                      • Children six months to five years old
     § Usually no human immunity
     § Happens only occasionally                                                           • Adults 50 years or older

                                                                                           • Chronic illness

                                                                                   • Pathogenic wind (exterior conditions)
                                                                                     s Wind cold, Wind heat, Wind dampness
                                                                                     s

• Comparison of cold and flu sx

Sx           Cold                       Flu

fever        rare                       high (102-104 F)

H/A          rare                       prominent

aches/pains  slight                     usual, often severe

fatigue/ weakness mild                  can last 2-3 weeks

extreme exhaustion never                early and prominent

stuffy nose  common                     sometimes

sneezing     usual                      sometimes

sore throat  common                     sometimes

cough        hacking                    can be severe

chest discomfort mild to moderate common

• Complications
  s Common cold
     § Better w/i days to weeks
     § Sinus, ear infections; bronchitis
  s Influenza
     § Worsening of chronic medical conditions such as asthma, congestive heart

        failure, diabetes
     § Sinus, ear infections; bronchitis
     § Pneumonia; dehydration; death

• Peak Month of Flu Activity: February

• Humidity and cold viruses
  s Cold-causing viruses survive better when humidity is low.
  s Cold weather also can dry the mucus membranes of the nose, making it

     more vulnerable to infection.

     and herbal therapy.                                                             s Gui Zhi Tang: wind cold; effective for very acute conditions w/ qi deficiency

                                                                                     s Bu Fei Wan: lung qi and yin deficiency (Good for people who have suffered
     is used as medicine.
                                                                                        major losses)
• Flavors and organ systems
  s “Sourness enters the Liver,                                                    • TCM cold and flu prevention tips
     § [Sour à Pucker à Contraction à LV Qi Stagnation]                              s Wash hands!
     § Little bit of lemon juice in water is good to kickstart digestion             s Don’t cough or sneeze into hands.
  s Bitterness enters the Heart,                                                     s Avoid touching face w/o washing hands afterwards
     § Dark greens à Build blood                                                     s Massage LI 20 every day; moxa ST 36
     § Coffee à Little bit lifts Heart Qi, but too much exhausts Heart Qi            s Drink plenty of water, and drink green tea!
  s Sweetness enters the Spleen                                                      s Eat food high in nutritional value; avoid low nutritional value foods.
     § Fruit                                                                         s Take saunas.
     § Rice, chicken (sweetest of meats), squash                                     s Exercise, especially outdoors, even if it’s raining (but do get dry and warm
     § Too much sweet goes to middle & sticks there à Creates Damp
  s Acridity enters the Lungs                                                           afterwards!).
     § [Pungent: wasabi] à Goes up and out                                           s Practice qi gong.
  s Saltiness enters the Kidneys.                                                    s Don’t smoke.
     § Leaches & drains, sucks water out & drains away à dries                       s Avoid alcohol.
     § Too much salt dries it out à Can drain KD Qi                                  s Relax, find enjoyment in little things
     § A little bit of salt (seafood) tonifies KD                                    s Wear warm clothes in cold weather.
     § Lamb: Yang food                                                               s Keep your feet warm (Tonifies Spleen & Kidney)
  s Sourness enters the sinews,                                                      s Eat chicken soup and mushroom soup, esp. w/ huang qi.
  s Bitterness enters the Blood, [Heart is emperor of Blood]                         s Ventilate living and working environments with fresh air.
  s Acridity enters Qi,                                                              s Avoid crowds.
  s Sweetness enters the flesh,
  s Saltiness enters the bones.” Huang Di Nei Jing

  s Yin Xu: Recommend Cool

• General dietary principles & Herbal formulas

• Characterized by the reverse flow of Lung qi

• Primarily results from diseases of the Lung, or dysfunction of Liver, Spleen, or
  Kidney

• External invasion of Lung by the six exogenous pathogens: wind, damp, cold,
  heat, dryness, summerheat

• Internal injury caused by deficiency of Lung qi and yin, Spleen qi, Kidney qi
  and yin, or by Liver fire attacking the Lung

• Lung is tender Zang and doesn’t like Heat

Shen-nong.com Analysis: in traditional Chinese medicine (TCM) theory, the bladder meridian governs the body surface. When external wind and cold invade the

body, the meridian is usually affected, resulting in discomforts along its pathway such as pain in the head and neck. The wind and cold pathogens hinder the

protective qi flowing on the body surface, so there are aversion to wind, chills and general aches. The tongue coating and the pulse also indicate that the pathogens

are located in the exterior portion of the body.

Placing more than one needle in a channel, it is better for draining that entire channel [Helps when they’re within some proximity]

Order of needling along the flow of Qi

Electro should be within 3 cun for best effects

• Broad range (~150) of recurrent emotional and physical sx 7-14 days prior to      • PMS-C (C = carbohydrate craving)

  menstruation                                                                            fainting, and heart palpitations

• Typically presents with decreased energy, tension, irritability, depression,      • Glucose tolerance tests during the 5-10 days before menses suggest excessive
  headache, altered sex drive, breast pain, backache, abdominal bloating, and          secretion of insulin in response to sugar consumption; during other parts of
  edema of extremities                                                                 the menstrual cycle GTT is normal.

Prevalence of PMS                                                                   PMS-D (D = depression) [Liver, Kidney]
• Affects between 30 and 40% of menstruating women                                     s Least common
• Peak occurrences among women in their 30s and 40s                                    s Key symptom is depression associated with low levels of neurotransmitters
• Severe in about 10% of women, then referred to as premenstrual dysphoric                in CNS
                                                                                       s Likely due to increased breakdown of neurotransmitters d/t decreased levels
  disorder                                                                                of estrogen
Abraham's PMS classifications                                                          s Decreased ovarian estrogen output has been attributed to a stress-induced
                                                                                          increase in adrenal androgen and/or progesterone secretion.
• (Abraham GE. Nutritional factor in the etiology of the premenstrual tension
  syndromes. J Reprod Med 1983; 28: 446-464)                                        • PMS-H (H = hyperhydration) [Damp, KD, SP]

  s Most common symptom category                                                          the face, hands and ankles

• Definition                                                              THERAPEUTICS 1
  s Cessation of menstruation, most commonly around age 50
  s 6-12 months without menses                                              Menopause
  s Hot flashes, a characteristic sign occurring in 65-80% of women
  s The time close and leading up to menopause is referred to as                      • Hormone Replacement Therapy (HRT)
                                                                                         s Robert A. Wilson MD published Feminine Forever in 1966.
     perimenopause; afterwards is referred to as postmenopausal.                         s Menopause is an estrogen deficiency disease to be treated with drugs

• Symptoms                                                                            • Benefits of HRT
  s Irregular menstruation                                                               s Relief of hot flashes and other menopausal symptoms
  s Hot flashes                                                                          s Reduction in osteoporosis
  s Mental irritability and depression                                                   s Some protection against heart disease and strokes
  s Hypertension and chest pain
  s Atrophic vaginitis                                                                • Side-effects of HRT
  s Frequent urinary tract infections                                                    s Breast cancer
  s Osteopenia or osteoporosis                                                           s Breast tenderness
  s Cold hands and feet                                                                  s PMS sx

TCM Dermatology

Introduction

• Viewpoint of TCM dermatology: For every skin (exterior) condition, there must be something wrong on the inside [Interior of body affects exterior of body]

• TCM dermatology is very effective
• Not enough western medicine dermatologists à Takes a long time to get an appointment

Pathology

Main Organs:                                                                      Acute Stage

• LU [Lung, skin & hair all related]                                              • Wind Heat [Red, wind is fast]
• LI [very important role in skin conditions] à One of most important organs in     s Itching à Wind
                                                                                    s Heat à Red
  dermatology                                                                       s When chronic condition flares up, it’s Wind Heat
  s Slumber parties as a kid [Constipation from bad food, staying up late à
                                                                                  • Damp Heat [Red & oozing à sometimes can see water underneath]:
     toxins accumulate è Toxins overflow to skin]                                   s Damp à Wet, pus [white or yellow]
  s Too much alcohol
  s Too much spicy food, barbecue, etc.                                           • Blood Heat [Toxin]
                                                                                    s Toxin à Heat [Chemotherapy: puts toxins directly into blood flow à
• ST [Very important], SP:

• HT [Expresses on face, in charge of blood. When blood has too much heat &       poisoned]

toxins, it circulates throughout body],

• LV: Blue-green [Hepatitis B or C: skin looks bluish, spider-like (late stage)]  Chronic Stage [Pale, dry, flaky]

                                                                                  • Dry [Wind Dry]: Fluid is gone, wetness gone

                                                                                    s Wind blow up, then makes dry & flaky

                                                                                  • Fluid Xu

                                                                                  • Blood Xu

                                                                                  • Yin Xu [Deficiency Heat symptoms]

Treatment

Treat Internal & External à Remove the pathology [Root of problem]

• Topically treated with acupuncture plus herbs [wash, plaster, powder]

• Internally treated with acupuncture & herbs

• TCM treats skin disorders from both inside & outside of body [Advantage & characteristics of TCM]

• Western Medicine focuses on local with biopsy, culture, etc.

  shape or with pimples rising one after another                                   • Formula: Xiao Feng San [Dispersing Wind]

• Hives may appear on any part of body with one or many areas                      Supplementary Points

• Rash may last for a few minutes or several days                                  Promote bowel movements:

• Rarely, hives may cause problems with breathing, creating danger of a severe     • Avoid heat or rubbing, which releases more histamines
  asthma attack or a closing of throat from swelling                               • Take antihistamine medicine to reduce allergic response

• Divided into following types according to clinical manifestations:               • For emergency cases that cause hives to spread quickly, may require a shot

Types              Clinical Manifestations                                           of antihistamine such as Benadryl
                                                                                   • If any suspicion that throat is swelling or patient is having trouble breathing
Wind Heat          Red Rashes with severe itching.
(Wind à quick)     Fever, chills [Fever >> Chills] sore throat                       or wheezing, administer epinephrine (adrenaline) to counteract reaction.
                   P: Floating & rapid                                             • For life threatening cases, call 911 or refer to ER.

Wind Damp          White or light red rashes accompanied by heaviness of
                   body [Looks wet, water inside]
                   T: White & sticky tongue coat
                   P: Floating & slow pulse

Heat accumulates Red rashes accompanied by abdominal or epigastric pain,
in ST & Intestines constipation or diarrhea

• Food allergies   • Diarrhea: Intestines inflamed due to allergies, but still
• Parasites          have room for the water
•
                   • Constipation: Intestines swollen inward à close up,
                     nothing can pass through

                   T: Thin, yellow coat
                   P: Rapid

Diagnosis
Find out history of sensitivity to such things as:

• Food [Especially shellfish, eggs, milk, nuts, berries, dyes or other additives]
• Plants [nettles], pollens

• Medicines [Penicillin, aspirin, sulfa]
• Animal fur, saliva
• Insect bites, stings

• Exposure to heat, cold, sunshine

Additional Considerations
• Easiest to identify drugs, foods or plants that may cause hives because the

  response usually occurs within an hour.
• Triggers such as emotional stress or multiple allergies may take more time &

  require skin tests or other types of tests.
• Suggest that patient keep a detailed diary of anything they eat, drink or are

  exposed to for 2-4 weeks
• Avoid suspect foods, then add back in one at a time
• See allergist for skin tests

Etiology

• During teenage years, hormones stimulate growth of body hair & oil glands

secrete more oil. Skin pores become clogged & bacteria grow in clogged

pores.

• When body works to kill the bacteria, whiteheads, blackheads & pustules form

in these areas.

• Stress, birth control pills & not enough sleep can make acne worse

  s Main characteristics: pain, fever, chills, edema? Chief feature: Itching! Symmetrical distribution
  s Chronic eczema: what most effective? Moxa!
  s Acute eczema usually caused by what? Wind cold, wind fire? Types of acute eczema
• Psoriasis
  s Where usually occurs? Yang areas? Top head, scalp, elbow, knees,
  s Which skin condition aggravated with emotional stimulation? Which is most easily flared up with emotional? Psoriasis
  s Main yang organ treated with excess skin disorders [Large Intestine]
• Expect quick resolution of alopecia: scalp massage, 7 star needle, hair growing lotion à expect effects in 2 weeks
• Alopecia: Yin organ not involved: Heart
• Besides SJ 6: other points to treat constipation UB 25, ST 37, SP 8, etc.
• In chronic stage of dermatological conditions: What kind of deficiency? Chronic skin condition pathology à review
• Acne: teenagers: Yin lags with yang excess
• Hair starts falling gradually due to emotions? Anger
• Case studies: urticaria, eczema, herpes zoster à know features & points
• Need diagnosis first, if wrong, everything else will be wrong
• Skin condition in TCM usually manifestation of internal organ disorders
• TCM treats skin conditions both topically and internally
• Not main pathology of TCM à Acute skin condition pathology [introduction page]
• What combo used to promote Qi & Blood production: SP 6 & ST 36
• Combo clear excess heat: LI 11 & DU 14

ADDITIONAL NOTES

Therapeutics 1: Common diseases (headaches, PMS)
Therapeutics 2: Systems (musculoskeletal, skin (dermatological), etc.)
Therapeutics 3: Cardiology, cardiovascular, etc.
Therapeutics 4: Gynecological
Therapeutics 5:
Therapeutics 6: EENT (Eyes, ears, nose, throat)
Therapeutics 7: Male disorders, endocrine

9/28/2016

Essence of TCM (Zhong Yong Zhi Dao): Moderation in everything
Chinese Medicine: Focus on Balance!!!!
• When balanced, immunity is strong!

Quiz Questions

• What is Bi?

• Bi since it’s so big = Rheumatology Department in Western Medicine

• Symptoms of Bi: Swelling, heat, puffiness, limited range of motion, atrophy (loss of joint function)

• Differential diagnosis

• How many diagnostic systems? 4
  s Etiology (know how it started)
  s Tissues (don’t know cause, but know affected tissue)
     § Tendon, muscle, bone, skin
  s Organ systems (when it gets into organ, it’s deeper because the organ is the root/foundation of channels à problem not only in meridian system) à Severe
     § Have organ symptoms: Bi + organ symptoms, deeper, severe

        ∗ Lung: breathing difficulty, coughing)

        ∗ Liver: anxiety, depression, mood swings
        ∗ Kidney: Lot of things, last resort à water metabolism, urinary dysfunction, sexual dysfunction, memory loss
  s Manifestation
     § Wandering Bi: Wind, always moving
     § Painful Bi: Cold
     § Fixed Bi: Pain fixed, puffy, worse with humidity, rainy days
     § Heat
     § Severe

Pain Questions
• Property/Nature: Dull, sharp, throbbing, stabbing, aggravating/alleviating

Case Study
• 1st Sentence: Read carefully for chief complaint
• Skip to Pulse & tongue à Gives truth (Especially Pulse)

Broken sleep à no good
•

Vomiting                                                                         • Western Medicine: Vomiting
• Vomiting: Forceful expulsion of contents of stomach through mouth &            • Occurs when nerves in body or brain sense a trigger (food poisoning, certain

  sometimes nose                                                                   infections, medicines, motion)
• Not regurgitation: normal behavior for birds [feed babies], no stomach upset   • Due to a large number of causes
• TCM organs: LV, SP, ST                                                         • Protracted vomiting has a long differential diagnosis
• Chinese word: Content with sound, Discontent with no sound                     • Usually a sign of gastritis or poisoning, or as a non-specific sequelae of

Newborn & Infants: 2 conditions                                                    disorders ranging from brain tumors & elevated intracranial pressure to
                                                                                   overexposure to ionizing radiation
Happy Spitter                    • Lower esophagus valve tightens up             General Symptoms & Signs
                                                                                 • Nausea, sweating, pale face, tachycardia, Area Postrema
                                 sometime in first year, usually around 4-         s BM: Loose with fever à Indicates gastroenteritis [GI system infection]
                                                                                   s Vomiting with abdominal pain & stop BM: Could indicate Peritonitis
[Should be gone after a year]    5 months [spitting up may go away]
                                                                                      § Abdomen feels hard (infection of membrane)
                                 • Content, no discomfort                        • Vomit color

                                 • Growing                                         s Bright Red (Hematemesis): Possible bleeding from esophagus
                                                                                   s Dark Red with liver-like clots: Profuse bleeding in stomach
                                 • No breathing problems from vomiting             s Coffee grounds: Less severe bleeding in stomach
                                                                                   s Yellow: Suggests bile
Gastroesophageal Reflux [GERD] • Discomfort, pain, crying
                                                                                 • Ask History
[Usually < 1 year old]           • Breathing problems [acid may go into            s How long you have ST pain?
Esophageal muscles not             trachea]                                        s Ulcer: long time (perforated ST wall)

developed                        • Poor growth                                   Other Treatment
                                                                                 • Auricular: ST, LV, Sympathetic, Subcortex, Shen Men
                                 • Should hold baby upright after eating or      • Extra: Zhong Kui [Middle finger knuckle crease]

                                 lay them down on side or stomach                Cautions:
                                                                                 • Aspiration of Vomit: Can be very dangerous if gastric content enters
Children
                                                                                   respiratory tract
• Gastroenteritis: Stomach flu                                                   • Vomiting can be beneficial to rid the body of harmful substances

• Food allergies, irritations [eggs, milk, seafood, nuts, shellfish]               s Medicines & methods to induce vomiting (ipecac syrup, finger in throat) are
                                                                                      recommended when necessary
• Anxiety & stress: Worries, tension, pressures of homework

• Flu, other illness: Seasonal flu, swine flu

• Eating too much

• Food poisoning

Adult: 4 Categories [Reflex, Central, Vestibular disorders, Neurotic]

Reflex

• Causes in Digestive Tract
  s Gastritis, Gastroenteritis
  s Gastroesophageal reflux disease [GERD]
  s Pyloric stenosis, Bowel obstruction, Ileus [Lack of movement in intestines]
  s Overeating
  s Acute abdomen and/or peritonitis
  s Food allergies, Food poisoning
  s Cholecystitis, pancreatitis, appendicitis, hepatitis
  s Milk allergy or lactose intolerance

• Self Induced
  s Eating disorders
  s Eliminate an ingested poison
  s Binge drinking: make room for more alcohol
  s Alleviate nausea

CNS Type
• Concussion, Cerebral hemorrhage, Brain tumors
• Hyperemesis [Morning sickness]
• Chemotherapy
• Norovirus [very contagious!!!] à Clean office well if someone vomits!!!
• Swine flu [Remedy: Ba Jiao (Star Anise) à good for viral infections]
• Adrenal insufficiency
• Uremia
• Metabolic disturbances
• Hypercalcemia
• Drug reaction
• Extreme pain [intense headache], migraine
• Post-operative

Labyrinth [Vestibular] Disorder
• Motion Sickness
• Meniere’s Disease

Neurotic Type
• Anxiety, Depression, violent emotions
• Disagreeable sights or disgust, smells or thoughts

THERAPEUTICS 3

Constipation                                                                        Newborns
Definiton
• Difficulty during defecation                                                      • Before 28 days
• Sensation of incomplete bowel evacuation                                            s Formula milk
• Infrequent bowel movements [< 3 times per week]                                        § Breast milk is easier to absorb, formula milk is difficult to absorb
• Includes obstruction & fecal impaction                                                 § Calcium can cause constipation
• Can become life threatening                                                            § Not enough glucose (sugar)/carbohydrates, which aids peristalsis
                                                                                      s Insufficient breast feeding or vomiting
Rome III Criteria                                                                        § Baby absorbs everything à nothing left for bowels
[At least 2 of below conditions within 12 weeks over 12 month period]                 s If weight is in normal range, no need to worry
• < 3 bowel movements/week                                                            s If underweight: Need to take action
• > 25% hard stool                                                                    s Mom’s Diet: Eating too much spicy food, etc.
• > 25% Sensation of incomplete bowel movements
• > 25% Difficult bowel movments                                                    • After 28 days
• > 25% Manual maneuvers to facilitate                                                s Unreasonable food:
• Insufficient criteria for IBS                                                          § Peristalsis: Protein slows, carbohydrates assist
                                                                                         § Intestines need to be more acidic than alkaline
• Gender: F > M [Yin Deficiency due to menstruation]                                     § Yogurt/Kefir help intestine to become more acidic à soft stool
• Age: Older > Younger [Yang deficiency, insufficient peristalsis (Function)]            § Eating too much meat, not enough fiber
                                                                                         § Cow milk is more difficult for a human to absorb
Etiology & Pathology                                                                  s Lack of good bowel habits
• Deficient Conditions                                                                   § Should be in morning: 5-7 AM
                                                                                         § Intestines need to learn schedule
  s Wei Qi is born in lower jiao, grows up in middle jiao and works in upper jiao        § Kids often suppress the intention [Want to play & not go to bathroom]
     [LU], when get sick, Wei Qi helps to repair. When sick for too long, SP isn’t    s Psychological factors
     able to function properly to create clear Qi à Blood Xu (impairs LI)             s Disease: Can cause constipation
     § No SP Blood Xu

• Amount of stool:                                                                    s Strong smell: Ask mother what she eats

  s Compare to amount of food eaten the previous day                                • Baby losing weight: Ask mother what she eats
     § If obviously less à Considered constipation

  s Length: Banana length is good                                                   • Formula milk constipation
  s Normal: 1-2 per day, more or less is not as normal                                s Difficult to absorb à Stool could be hard, yellow, strong smell

  s Eat more vegetables à Less smell                                                Adult Constipation

THERAPEUTICS 3

Diarrhea                                                                        Adult Diarrhea

• Definition: 3 or more loose or liquid bowel movements per day                 • Etiology & Pathology
• Zang Fu: SP, LV, KD                                                             s Bacterial Infections: Contaminated food or water
                                                                                  s Viral Infections: Rotavirus, norovirus, cytomegalovirus, herpes simplex
• When see babies, always touch fontanel to see if it’s closed & the quality         virus, viral hepatitis
• Should be closed by 2 years old, if not, there may be a problem                 s Parasites: Enter through food or water
• Back fontanel should be closed within a few months                              s Functional Bowel Disorders: Irritable bowel syndrome symptom
                                                                                  s Intestinal Diseases: Inflammatory bowel disease, ulcerative colitis, Chron’s
Infant diarrhea                                                                      disease, celiac disease
• First stool of newborn looks like diarrhea (meconium) [debris from amniotic     s Food Intolerance & Sensitivities: Lactose, sugar
                                                                                  s Reaction to Medicines: Antibiotics
  fluid]: Normal, not diarrhea
• Etiology & Pathology                                                          • Symptoms & Signs
                                                                                  s BM frequency increased significantly
  s Non-infective: Feeding too much, not enough, too much sugar                   s Stool: loose, sour odor, yellow color, mucus, undigested food
     § Cold invading abdomen (keep abdomen covered, even on hot days)             s Abdominal pain, anal heavy sensation, burning anus
     § Too Hot: stomach acid in stomach is not enough to digest food              s Abdominal pain alleviates after bowel movements
                                                                                  s Sinking or floating?
  s Infectious: Virus, bacteria, parasite                                            § If sinking, does it stick to bowl? Yes à Damp Heat
     § Sucking on unclean thumb, toys, etc.
                                                                                • Diagnosis through Stool
• Signs & Symptoms                                                                s Grey (no bilirubin from GB à blockage): GB stones, tumors, roundworm
  s Dehydration                                                                      § Pancreatic cancer: Itchy skin very common (bilirubin goes to skin instead
     § Urinating less often than normal, Dry mouth                                      of stool & causes itching), often not found until late stage
     § Irritability, No tears when crying                                         s Black: Upper GI Bleeding
     § Unusual drowsiness or lethargy                                             s Red: Lower GI bleeding
     § Sunken soft spot on top of baby’s head                                     s Tarry: Dysentery
     § Skin that isn’t as elastic as usual                                        s Yellow: Poor digestion of fats
  s General                                                                       s Foam & sour smell: Poor digestion of sugar
     § Fever of 102 degrees Fahrenheit or higher                                  s Rancid odor: Poor digestion of protein, harmful bacteria in intestine
     § Abdominal pain [Crying, cry more when touch abdomen]                       s Parasites or insects: Can be directly seen in stool
     § Blood or pus in stool, or stool is black, white or red
     § Vomiting                                                                 TCM Etiology & Pathology

• Treatments                                                                    • External Pathogens: Wind, Summer Heat, Damp, Cold, Heat
  s Don’t recommend OTC anti-diarrheal medicines for infants                    • Dietary Injury: Overeating
  s Diarrhea is very dangerous for babies à Recommend going to doctor
  s Dehydration: May need to receive IV fluids in hospital                      • LV Qi Stagnation Overacting on Spleen: Anger [Fear, anxiety]
                                                                                  s Also Alternating diarrhea & constipation
Extra Points
• Xie Xie Xue: Between GB 41 & GB 42                                            • SP/ST Weakness: Long Term illness
• Li Nei Ting: Opposite of ST 44 (bottom of foot)                               • KD Yang Deficiency: Old age, long term illness
• Auricular: Rectum, LI, Constipation, Sub Cortex, Sympathetic
                                                                                  s 5 AM diarrhea (Cock’s crow diarrhea)
Dietary
• Recommend only eating fully cooked food, warm foods                           TCM Signs, Symptoms & Treatments
• Congee, noodles
• Oral rehydration: Salt:Sugar 1:3                                              • Excess condition: abdominal pain with diarrhea, dislikes pressure
• High carbohydrates                                                            • Deficiency condition: May or may not have abdominal pain, not as severe pain,
• Zinc & potassium supplementation (only in infants over 6 months old): Banana
• Lactose intolerance: take digestive enzymes containing lactase when             likes pressure

  consuming dairy products
• Applesauce, blueberries
• Avoid caffeine & alcohol [Damp Heat]
• Avoid allergy causing foods: Milk, high fat, high gas
• Avoid insoluble fiber [90% of celery is water à highly recommended]
• Low protein for pancreatic diarrhea

THERAPEUTICS 3

Hypochondriac Pain                                                              Condition                       Excess               Deficiency
                                                                                Duration                        Rapid onset          Slow Onset
• TCM Zang Fu: LV & GB, SP & ST, KD                                             Pressure                        Acute, short         Chronic, long
                                                                                Physical Condition              Refuse, dislike      Prefer
• Hypochondriac Pain: Condition of a disorder characterized by pain over the    Pulse                           Strong               Weak
                                                                                Treatment Principle             Excess               Deficiency
  unilateral or bilateral sides of the hypochondriac region                                                     • Move Qi & Blood    • Nourish Yin & Blood
  s Pain in Right ribs different pathology from pain in Left ribs                                               • Clear Heat & Damp  • Soothe Liver
                                                                                                                • Reduce             • Tonify
     § Left Hypochondrium: Food stagnation, LV Qi Stagnation, Blood Stasis,

        phlegm fluid retention, Qi Stagnation
     § Right Hypochondrium: Phlegm fluid retention, food stagnation (can also

        cause LU issues), Qi Stagnation, LV Fire invading LU, Damp Heat

• Western Etiology & Pathology [WM: LV on Left only, TCM: Both Sides]
  s Liver cirrhosis, cholecystitis, hepatitis
  s Biliary tract infections: Leads to inflammation of pancreas [pancreatitis]
  s Intercostal neuralgia [12 nerves running between ribs]: Lung TB, Shingles

Differentiation  Hypochondriac Pain                              Stomach Pain                                       Chest Pain
Area             • Unilateral or bilateral Hypochondriac         • ST & Hypochondriac area                          • Behind Left anterior of chest
                                                                                                                    • Radiation to hypochondriac, Left shoulder,
Pain Character     regions & ST region
                                                                                                                      left back & left arm [HT Disease]

Dysmenorrhea                                                                     Etiology & Pathogenesis
Primary dysmenorrhea: About half of menstruating women. Causes:
• Contraction-causing chemicals [prostaglandins, arachidonic acid à induce       • Cramps occur from contractions in uterus [part of menstrual process]
                                                                                   s Uterus is very squishy, wants to get rid of blood & membrane
  myometrium (uterine muscle) contractions]
• Abnormal nervous system response: Some women may have autonomic                • Emotional Stimulation

  nervous systems that are overly sensitive to menstrual cycle changes           • Exogenous factors
  s Autonomic nervous system regulates heart rate, blood pressure & contains     • Qi & Blood Stagnation [Qi Stagnation à Blood Stagnation à Blood Stasis]

     pain receptors in nerve fibers in uterus & pelvic area                      • Chong & Ren Damage [Cramps more severe with a lot of bleeding disorders,
• Abnormalities in the arteries in the uterus                                      deep black bleeding, clots (internal damage)]
                                                                                   s Chong & Ren are very deep and big, like a reservoir
  s Doppler ultrasounds show impaired blood flow through uterine arteries in       s Damage from surgeries [ovarian cyst, tumors, fibroids, pregnancy
     women with severe dysmenorrhea                                                   termination]
                                                                                   s Sexual abuse [Very common in US]
• Genetic factors may play critical role in >50% of primary dysmenorrhea cases.
  s Onset usually occurs 2-3 years after periods have started                    Other Therapies

Uterine Bleeding                                                                 Western Medicine Diagnosis
                                                                                 • Endometrial biopsy: Sample of tissue from inside of uterus examined under
• Beng Lou: Uterine bleeding beyond the menstrual period, either copious or
  continuously spotting                                                            microscope
  s Beng: Sudden onset & profuse bleeding                                        • Ultrasound: Sound waves get pictures of uterus, ovaries & pelvis
  s Lou: Insidious onset & scantly bleeding                                      • Sonohysterogram: Ultrasound performed after fluid injected through a tube

• One is acute & the other chronic, but they often transform into each other       into uterus to look for problems with uterine lining (fibroids)
  s Profuse bleeding consumes Qi & Blood (Beng) à Continuous scanty bleeding     • Hysteroscope: Thin metal tube with light & tiny camera inserted through the
     (Lou)
  s Prolonged scanty bleeding (Lou) [Qi deficiency] à profuse bleeding (Beng)      vagina & cervix into uterus to look inside
                                                                                 • Laparoscopy: Thin metal tube with light & tiny camera inserted into abdomen

Western Medicine Causes                                                            s Endometrial ablation: Using a laser, electrocautery instrument or thermal
• Hormone imbalance: Most common cause. Sometimes caused by improper
                                                                                      balloon to destroy the uterine lining
  use of hormone medicine                                                          s Hysterectomy: Removal of uterus
  s Should only use hormones as a last resort because they can interfere with

     normal development
  s Much lower rates of breast & ovarian cancer in China because they don’t use

     so many hormones
  s Aging is healthy. Don’t fight it. Don’t try to extend the length of life.

• Fibroids: Non-cancerous growths in uterus [Grows in muscle]

• Affects about 1 of 5 couples in USA                                               • Post-Coital Test: Cervical mucus examined after intercourse, including mucus
• Etiologic factors                                                                   receptivity & ability of sperm to survive & access upper reproductive tract.

  s Sperm disorders: 35% of couples                                                 Assisted Reproductive Techniques
  s Ovulatory dysfunction: 20%
  s Tubal dysfunction: 30%                                                          • Intrauterine insemination (IUI): Involves placing sperm inside a woman’s
  s Abnormal cervical mucus: 5%                                                       uterus to facilitate fertilization. Goal: increase number of sperm that reach
  s Unidentified factors: 10%                                                         fallopian tubes à increase the chance of fertilization. Provides sperm an
• Diagnosis & treatment require thorough assessment of both partners.                 advantage by giving it a head start, but still requires a sperm to reach and
• Financial burden & time commitment for diagnostic & therapeutic infertility         fertilize the egg on its own. Less invasive & less expensive option than IVF.
  procedures can cause marital strife.
                                                                                    • In Vitro Fertilization [IVF]: Used for patients with tubal disease,
• Failure to conceive often generates frustration, emotional stress, feelings of      endometriosis, ogliosperma, sperm antibodies, unexplained infertility.
  inadequacy, anger, guilt & resentment à Couples counseling & psychological          Procedure involves ovarian hyperstimulation, oocyte retrieval, fertilization,
  support are important adjuncts to treatment. Despite all efforts, some couples      embryo culture & embryo transfer to uterus. Despite transfer of multiple
  do not achieve pregnancy. They should be counseled about when to stop               embryos (often 3-4), average term pregnancy rate is 20-25% per attempt.
  treatments & consider adoption.
                                                                                    • Gamete Intrafallopian Tube Transfer [GIFT]: Used for women who have
                                                                                      normal tubal function but unexplained infertility or endometriosis. Multiple
                                                                                      oocytes & sperm are obtained like for IVF but transferred (laparoscopically or
                                                                                      transvaginally with ultrasound guidance) to distal fallopian tubes, where
                                                                                      fertilization occurs. Success rate: 25-35% at most infertility centers.

                                                                                    • Intrascytoplasmic Sperm Injection: Primary method of achieving pregnancy
                                                                                      when other techniques have not resulted in fertilization or when semen
                                                                                      analysis detects marked abnormalities. Sperm are injected into oocyte,
                                                                                      bypassing sperm abnormalities that affect attachment to or penetration into
                                                                                      oocyte.

                                                                               • Hyperemesis Gravidarum: Uncontrollable nausea & vomiting during
• Symptoms including nausea, vomiting, anorexia, dizziness during early
  pregnancy [often within first trimester of gestation]                          pregnancy that results in dehydration & acidosis. Distinct from ordinary
                                                                                 morning sickness with nausea & vomiting. Many pregnant women with
• Severe cases:                                                                  morning sickness feel that they are vomiting everything they ingest, but if they
  s Hyperemesis gravidarum                                                       continue to gain weight & aren’t dehydrated, they do not have hyperemesis
  s May emaciate the pregnant woman & lead to other disorders                    gravidarum. Persistent hyperemesis gravidarum is uncommon but may be
                                                                                 associated with serious LV damage.
• Common complaint in early stage of pregnancy & usually subsides between      • Treatment: Acidosis & dehydration corrected with IV fluids, gluose,
  week 12-16                                                                     electrolytes. Patient should be kept in bed in a hospital & not given anything
                                                                                 by mouth for 24 hours. Anti-emetics & sedatives used as necessary.

                                                    Angina Pectoris [Xiong Bi]

• Definition: Angina or Angina Pectoris                                           Western Medicine Classification
  s Chest pain due to ischemia of heart muscle
                                                                                  • Stable Angina [Effort Angina]
  s Most common symptom of coronary artery disease                                  s Definition: Refers to symptoms related to some activity with minimal or

                                                                                  non-existent symptoms at rest or with administration of sublingual

• Gender: M > F                                                                        nitroglycerin [Heart muscles need more oxygen]
• Age: Older > Younger [52-57]                                                      s Exhibits a definite pattern [Bad news may exacerbate]
                                                                                    s Commonly induced by exercise
  s People that suffer from average to severe cases of angina have an increased     s Generally relieved after resting
     percentage of death before the age of 55, usually around 60%                   s Pain usually lasts 3-5 minutes, medications help relieve pain
                                                                                    s Pain Experienced in chest may sometimes spread to other parts of body
Western Medicine: Etiology, Pathology, Manifestations
• Most common cause: Coronary Artery Disease (CAD) by atherosclerosis                  [neck, shoulder, left upper back, left arm]
                                                                                    s Pale face, spontaneous sweating, cold, blood pressure changes
  [caused by deposition of cholesterol plaque on the endothelium, which makes
  the arteries narrow & stiff à impedes supply of oxygen to heart & causes        • Unstable Angina
  chest pain]                                                                       s Occurs at rest or with minimal exertion [Usually lasting > 10 min]
• Another cause: Coronary artery spasm. Results in reduction of adequate            s Chest pain can last 10-15 minutes
  supply of oxygenated blood to heart muscle cells                                  s Severe & new onset
  s Inadequate oxygen à Necrosis                                                    s Occurs with crescendo pattern
                                                                                    s Can’t be cured by rest or medications
Common causes                                                                       s NO regular predictable pattern like stable angina
• Age [M: About 55, W: About 65]                                                    s EKG Pattern: ST up
• Cigarette smoking                                                                 s Sound: More than 2 sounds [3-4 sounds]

• Dyslipidemia                                                                    • Variant Angina [Prinzmetal]
• Family history of premature cardiovascular disease                                s Narrowing of coronary arteries caused by contraction of smooth muscle
• Hypertension,

• Obesity [BMI > 30]                                                                   younger women (~55) à Can lead to heart attack
• Physical inactivity                                                               s More chest discomfort than actual pain

When to refer to ER?

• Any heart disease that could predispose to a dangerous arrhythmia

When to refer to ER:

• Hypoglycemia
  s Low blood sugar, trembling, weakness
  s Lack of coordination, drowsiness, confusion
  s Headache, dizziness, Double vision
  s Convulsions or unconsciousness

• Ketoacidosis [Dangerous]
  s Shortness of breath, Fruity smelling breath
  s Nausea, vomiting, Very dry mouth, confusion, coma

• Hyperosmolar Hyperglycemic Non-Ketotic Syndrome [HHNS]
  s Blood sugar level > 600 mg/dL
  s Dry, parched mouth
  s Extreme thirst [may gradually disappear]
  s Warm, dry skin with NO sweat
  s High fever (> 101F)

Glaucoma                                                                               Other Therapies
• Definition: An eye disease in which the flow of aqueous humor (clear, watery
                                                                                       • Herbs
  fluid that normally circulates in eye) is out of balance, with more fluid staying      s Dan Zhi Shao Yao Wan [Xiao Yao San + 2 heat clearing herbs]
  in the eye than exiting due to flow of fluid out of eye becomes blocked.               s Huang Lian Shang Qing Wan: Heat & toxins in GI, constipation
  Results in painful buildup of fluid in eyeball that creates pressure, leading to       s Qi Ju Di Huang Wan: Eye disorders caused by LV & KD Xu [nourish]
  impaired vision. Patients often report seeing halos around lights in earlier           s Zhi Bai Di Huang Wan: LV & KD Yin Xu with deficient heat
  stages. If untreated, the increased pressure can cause atrophy of the optic
  nerve and cause blindness.                                                           • Auricular
• TCM Name: Qing Feng Nei Zhang [Green Wind Internal Blockage]                           s LV, KD, Eye, Sympathetic, Subcortics, Forehead
• Main Organs: LV, KD, SP                                                                s Same side as eye problems [L eye à L ear]
                                                                                         s Needles or seeds
Etiology & Pathogenesis                                                                  s Bleed on back of ear [vein runs perpendicular]: 3-5 drops of blood à
• Onset can be rapid & acute or slow & chronic
• Two main types, depending on how flow of fluid is blocked                                 pressure drops immediately, also works for hypertension
• Open Angle Glaucoma: Caused by gradual blockage in area that drains fluid                 § Distended veins à HBP
                                                                                            § Very high IOP: bleed vein, then send immediately to ER [try to save nerve]
  from eyes into drainage channels. More common type of glaucoma in US &
  Europe. Usually affects both eyes.                                                   • 7 star needle
• Closed Angle Glaucoma: (More dangerous, often because of injury or trauma)             s Mild tapping: Tai Yang, Yu Yao, UB 2, GB 14,
  Blockage cause by a change in the position of the iris (colored part of eye),          s Moderate Tapping: SP 6, UB 15 à UB 23
  which causes the iris to block the drainage channels. Usually happens in one
  eye at a time. If one eye gets glaucoma, the other eye is at risk for having the     Western Diagnosis
  same problem. When this type of glaucoma happens suddenly, it is called
  acute closed-angle glaucoma & is a medical emergency.                                • Comprehensive ophthalmic examination essential for diagnosis & prompt
  s Symptoms of acute closed angle glaucoma                                              treatment
  s Severe eye pain, Seeing halos around lights
  s Nausea & vomiting, Headache, Loss of vision                                        • Examination includes visualization of the angle by a special prism or contact
                                                                                         lens, IOP measurement, visual field examination, most importantly,
Differentiation
• Normal IOP [Intraocular pressure]: 11-21 mm/Hg                                         examination of optic disks
• Exceptions:
                                                                                       • Eye doctor may use the following test
  s Normal or low pressure glaucoma: Some people with normal pressure                    s Tonometry: Painless procedure used to measure pressure in eye
     develop optic nerve damage                                                          s Opthalmoscopy: Uses instrument to look at the optic nerve inside eye

## p.113

THERAPEUTICS 6: EYES, EARS, NOSE, THROAT [EENT]

Strabismus
Definition
• Strabismus: Also named squint, refers to disorder where the two eyes of

  patient area unable to stare straight simultaneously [eyes point in different
  directions.] Usually one eye pointed straight ahead & other pointed in, out, up
  or down.
• TCM: Wind blowing squint

Etiology & Pathogenesis
• Normal Vision: Brain fuses images from two eyes aimed at same target into

  one clear image [called binocular vision].
• Strabismus: Two different images sent to brain. Occurs when eye muscles are

  not balanced. Movements of muscles of one eye do not match those of other.
  s Young child: brain starts to ignore image from turned eye & child loses

     depth perception & vision in turned eye.
  s Adults: Often have double vision because the brain is used to receiving two

     images and cannot easily ignore image from turned eye.

Causes                                                                             Western View & Treatment
• LV & KD Deficiency                                                               Strabismus in adults may be caused by:
• Wind invasion into meridians around eye: Internal wind from high fever           • Strabismus from childhood that recurs
• Inadequate gesture                                                               • Poor vision in one eye, which begins to drift outward
• Congenital strabismus                                                            • Concussion (injury to brain)
• Trauma                                                                           • Injury to nerves that control eye muscle movement, sometimes as a result of
• Paralytic Strabismus: Muscular paralysis around eye
                                                                                     high blood pressure, thyroid disorders, diabetes, myasthenia gravis, MS,
  s Estropia [Eye turns IN] (Cross-eye): Abductor paralysis [internal strabismus]    inflammatory & infectious diseases, tumors, aneurysms
  s Extropia Eye turns OUT (walleye): Adductor paralysis [external strabismus]     Diagnosis: Eye doctor tests vision & ability to follow objects with each eye
  s Hypertropia: Inferior muscle paralysis, Eye turns UP                           Treatment: Goals: Restore vision, if amblyopia present, straighten eyes &
  s Hypotropia: Superior muscle paralysis, Eye turns DOWN                          restore the ability to make normal eye contact & restore binocular vision.
• Conjugate Strabismus: Most Difficult to treat                                    • In children, treatment should begin as soon as the condition is detected.
                                                                                     Treatment that begins after 6 may improve a child’s appearance but not
Differentiation                                                                      always the vision.
• All: Difficult movement                                                          • Treating any amblyopia by patching or using special eyedrops in the good eye,
• Squinting in one or both eyes, turning or tilting head, limited movement of one    which force the brain to pay attention to the weak eye.
                                                                                   • Glasses for farsightedness or to improve the focus of the eye. Farsighted
  or both eyes, diplopia                                                             children have to work harder to focus their eyes. [An eye with poor vision is
                                                                                     more likely to drift.] Glasses that correct the farsightedness help keep the
Treatment Principles                                                                 eyes from crossing while they are worn
• Regulate muscles of eyes                                                         • Eye exercises to train the eyes to move together & focus on the same object at
• Tonify LV & KD                                                                     the same time.
                                                                                   • Prism glasses for adults, which may eliminate double vision while they are
Point Prescription                                                                   being worn.
• Points: ST 2, ST 36, LI 4, GB 20                                                 • Surgery on the eye muscles. Muscles may be loosened, tightened, or
• Internal [lateral weak muscles]:                                                   repositioned. If this surgery is performed early enough in some children, they
                                                                                     may develop normal vision. Like any surgery, eye surgery has risks. Surgery
  s Tai Yang, GB1, SJ 23, Qiu Hou [stimulation on lateral side]                      success depends partly on whether coordination between the eyes & brain is
• External [medial weak muscles]:                                                    good enough to keep the eyes locked on target & in proper alignment.

  s UB 1, UB 2, Yu Yao, Shang Jing Ming, Xia Jing Ming

• Bloodletting: Bleed Ear apex for excess                                            energy.

                                                                                     regulating balance.

                                                                                  s Eustachian Tube: Important structure in ear, runs from middle ear to
                                                                                     passages behind nose & upper part of throat. Ventilates ear & equalizes air
                                                                                     pressure in middle ear to outside air pressure. Problems here are primary
                                                                                     factors in most ear infections.

Types of Hearing Loss                                                              Levels of Severity: Determined by loudness of sound that a patient can hear
• Conductive: Hearing loss results when something interferes with sound waves      without a hearing aid. Loudness is measured in decibels [dB]

  traveling through outer & middle parts of ear. Causes:                           • Mild: Can hear sounds of 20-40 dB or louder. May have trouble hearing faint
  s Complete blockage in outer ear by wax, infection (otitis media)                  or distant speech. Most common cause: fluid accumulation in middle ear
  s Collection of fluid in middle ear, damage to tiny bones in middle ear            (conductive hearing loss)
• Sensorineural: Results from a problem in the innermost part of the ear or
  auditory nerve. Causes:                                                          • Moderate: Can hear sounds of 45-60 dB or louder. Need speech to be loud.
  s Tumors, injury from diseases (meningitis, rubella)                               Difficulty understanding speech in group situations.
  s Physical injury to inner ear, abnormal development of inner ear
  s Children with both hearing & balance problems may have mild delays in          • Severe: Can only hear sounds of 65-85 dB or louder. Can hear only loud voices
                                                                                     one foot or less away, or loud sounds in environment.
     development of motor skills
• Early testing is important in helping a hearing impaired patient adapt.          • Profound: May hear loud sounds of 90 dB or louder, but may be more aware
                                                                                     of vibrations than sound. The term deaf usually applies to patients with
                                                                                     profound hearing losses or no hearing at all.

                                                                                   • Patients with hearing loss that crosses two levels of severity are given the label
                                                                                     of both (mild to moderate, moderate to severe)

Epistaxis                                                                          Other Therapies
• Epistaxis (nosebleed) occurs when the membranes lining the inner nose are
                                                                                   • Auricular: Inner LU, adrenal, KD, ST, Shen Men, Zero
  disturbed or irritated enough to cause abnormal bleeding.
• TCM: Bi Nu [Nu = bleeding]                                                       • Bleeding: LU 11, LI 1, UB 40
                                                                                     s 3-5 drops, get rid of heat
  s LU, ST, KD mainly involved
                                                                                   • Ancient Prescriptions
Common causes: Breakdown in membranes lining the nose                                s DU 23, UB 45, SJ 16, GB 20
• Can be triggered by dry air, probing, bumping, picking or rubbing nose             s 100 units moxa on KD 1 [10 more practical]
• Blowing nose forcefully can also cause nosebleed, especially if nasal              s Du 16, LI 2, LI 20

  membranes are already inflamed due to allergies or infection.                    • Herbs
• Injury to face or nose, high altitude, high blood pressure                         s Yun Nan Bai Yao [Best: Capsules, tincture]
• Re-bleeding from an area of previous nosebleed                                        § Lower back pain, knee pain
• Medicine that increases clotting time
• Nosebleed after heavy blow to skull could mean fractured skull à ER

First Aid

• When nose starts bleeding, sit up & lean forward to prevent blood from
  passing into throat

• Pinch nose firmly together between thumb & index finger, just below nasal
  bones, hold for 10 minutes.

• After bleeding stops, use saline nasal spray or nose drops to keep nose moist.

• Do not blow nose for several hours.
• If a nosebleed lasts > 20 minutes or does not respond to first aid à go to MD

  s Will likely apply cotton ball soaked in epinephrine, or nose drops like Neo-
     Synephrine or Afrin to bleeding site for 5-10 minutes to numb & temporarily
     reduce blood supply to nasal membrane.

  s Then pack nose with Vaseline coated gauze or special balloon to provide
     pressure

  s Can also use cauterization to force blood to coagulate at bleeding site. Will
     help prevent future bleeding.

• Definition: Contagious bacterial infection that involves lungs, but may spread
  to other organs [Can be anywhere in body]                                          s People who have had the immunization will have positive PPD
     § Agricultural countries: Most common is in knee                              • Chest X-ray
     § Developing countries: Can affect spine
                                                                                     s Early Lung TB mostly has no symptoms, is easy to overlook or delay
• Known since ancient Egypt. Most common mode of transmission in US is                  treatment and often is found inadvertently on chest X-ray
  inhalation of infected airborne droplets. Initial TB infection can manifest
  clinically as the disease, but usually presents with no symptoms because the       s Edges are clear on X-ray
  immune system controls the bacteria.                                               s Cancer does not have clear edges because of blood vessels surrounding it.

• Recrudescence (reactivation) of dormant bacteria is most common clinical

Benign Prostatic Hyperplasia                                                        Common BPH Symptoms: Usually start after age 50
                                                                                    • Trouble starting a urine stream or making more than a dribble
• Prostate Location: Walnut sized gland located between the bladder and penis       • Passing urine often, especially at night
  (only in dogs and adult human males), just in front of the rectum. The urethra    • Feeling that the bladder has not fully emptied

• Geographical                                                                    Treatment Principles [RRMS most frequently seen in clinic.]

• Dietary: Saturated fat & dairy (major component) à Damp.

Western Medicine Clinical Manifestation                                             s Base treatment on syndrome differentiation.

• Symptoms vary because the location and severity of each attack can be             s Treat with herbs to build up SP Qi, Resolve damp, Nourish LV & KD yin.

different. Episodes can last for days, weeks or months. These episodes

alternate with periods of reduced or no symptoms (remissions).

• Fever, hot baths, sun exposure and stress can trigger or worsen attacks.

• Relapses (return of the disease) are common. However, the disease may
  continue to get worse without periods of remission.

• Restless Leg Syndrome

• Signs: No redness, swelling, heat or scarring

• Age: Onset from 18 – 50 years old

• Gender: Affects predominantly women over men [Ratio: 8:1 or 9:1]

• Palpation: Extreme pain on tender spots from 4 kg force

• No range of motion limitation (joints)

• X-ray or other imaging: Normal

Western Medicine Diagnosis

• Joint & Muscle examination: Normal

• No laboratory abnormalities

• History of widespread pain for > 3 months

• Digital Palpation: At least 11 of 18 tender point sites

• Some patients have fewer tender sites and more regional pain

• Alternate diagnosis: Symptoms Score + fewer tender points
  s [Score of 5 and 7+ tender points] or [Score of 9 and 3-6 tender points]
  s Symptoms
     § Fatigue [0-3]
     § Restless after rest [0-3]
     § Forgetfulness [0-3]
     § Symptoms after rest [0-3]

Western Treatment

• Painkillers, anti-seizure drugs, anti-depressants [amitriptyline]
  s These drugs can affect sexual function in males
